# Global Strategy

# Quantamental Signal Ideas - Top & Bottom Ranked Stocks

Details on the methodology/model can be found in our Q-Series report

## 11 Quantamental signals to spot opportunities in global equities

We recently introduced our new quantamental framework for single stocks. This model uses 11 high-performing indicators that aim to extract information from price momentum, stocks’ fundamentals and investors’ sentiment/positioning... In this note, we flag stocks across five regions (US, EU, China, Japan and Australia) that screen well quantitatively in our framework and qualitatively based on UBS analysts’ ratings. An easy-to-read dashboard will help visualize the quantamental scores, while our analysts have shared short blurbs highlighting their fundamental views on the selected stocks.

## What are the model’s recommendations today?

Based on the stock-level scores and an optimisation for portfolio balance, we find that the highest-scoring stocks tend to come from sectors like Transportation, Banks, Auto Components and Energy. The lowest-scoring stock list is dominated by Health Care, Household Personal Products, Tech and Food Beverage & Tobacco stocks. Do note these recommendations can differ from the fundamental views of our equity strategy team.

## Digging into the details, what key message is each signal cohort suggesting?

• Chinese stocks are clearly lagging from a price momentum perspective. The most favoured sectors are Financials & Industrials, the least favoured are Real Estate & Utilities. • Positioning is very supportive in China, more balanced in other regions. Real estate is clearly the sector to overweight based on positioning, alongside with communication services. Energy is consistently ranked among the least favoured sectors.

• Earnings expectations and momentum seem to favour Real Estate (ex Japan) & Cons. Discretionary stocks. On the opposite side, Utilities & Materials are the least favoured. • With OECD G20 CLI indicating expansion as the most likely regime in 2024 (38% probability, vs. 15% for a downturn), cyclical sectors are favoured in our regime model. • Valuation wise, Financials and Utilities appear cheap in our adjusted 12m fwd PE model, when Tech and Health Care appear expensive. Please keep in mind there seem to be significant relative value plays within sectors and industries, and across regions.

• Sector PMI new orders suggest to favour Staples and Financials, over Health Care and Materials. Rankings appear to be quite consistent across regions.

## What are the industries whose rankings have changed the most?

Compared to the previous update, our model is slightly reducing its risk-on stance, albeit from a high level. Food, Beverage & Tobacco, Telecom and Banks have seen their overall scores upgraded, when Health Care, Utilities and Tech have seen their overall scores reduced, despite being the least favoured industries already.

UBS publishes a report that utilizes a quantamental framework for single stocks known as the "Quantamental Signals Strategy." The model uses 11 high-performing indicators to filter a trading signal for each stock globally. An easy-to-read dashboard that includes all stocks listed on the S&P 500, Eurostoxx 600, MSCI China, Nikkei 225 and S&P/ASX 200 is available, and you should contact your UBS sales representative or the Quantitative Research team at qa@ubs.com for details and pricing.

## Quick reminder of the 11 quantamental signals

<table><tr><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>i)</td><td>(Four) CTA Momentum Signals: aim to benefit from the persistence in asset prices. Our CTA trend-following algorithm is applied on 1) stock, 2) industry, 3) sector and 4) country index prices</td></tr><tr><td>ii)</td><td>(Three) Regime Probability Signals: rely on the principle that assets&#x27; prices are greatly influenced by economic regimes.</td></tr><tr><td>iii)</td><td>Estimating each regime probability and positioning accordingly can be a great source of alpha Crowding Signal: empirical work suggests we should participate during the building phase of a &#x27;consensus&#x27; trade and</td></tr><tr><td>iv)</td><td>only be contrarian - i.e. play short-term mean reversion - when positioning has become extreme EPS Growth (ML vs Consensus) Signal: attempt to identify stocks with the highest risk of upgrades/downgrades from</td></tr><tr><td></td><td>analysts. Surprises are powerful drivers of absolute and relative returns</td></tr><tr><td>v)</td><td>(Extreme) Rates Momentum Signal: speed and magnitude of bond moves are ones of the key factors of investors&#x27; risk appetite. Increasing equity risk after a strong rally or sell-off in rates has historically been rewarded</td></tr><tr><td>vi)</td><td>Risk Targeting Signal: risk targeting is an &#x27;optimized&#x27; long-only strategy that relies on two levers - volatilities and correlations - to produce superior returns</td></tr><tr><td>vii) viii)</td><td>Up&amp;Down Volatility Signal: is a price-based indicator of positioning that gauges the probability of a trend reversal Options Volumes Signal: is a leading indicator of investors&#x27; nervousness. Some investors seek to postpone the unpleasant</td></tr><tr><td>ix)</td><td>feeling of crystallizing losses using derivatives hedging. More often than not, it leads to capitulation</td></tr><tr><td>x)</td><td>Adjusted Forward PE Ratio Signal: value is a popular strategy for patient investors. We enhance one of the most popular valuation indictor, the fPE Ratio, by adjusting the numerator (i.e. prices) by the quality of the companies&#x27; balance sheets</td></tr><tr><td></td><td>Sector PMI (New Orders) Signal: relies on the same economic priors as the Regime Probability signal. The main difference is that rather than a single leading index, we utilise sector level data on a specific leading indicator that surveys businesses directly on their forward looking business activity</td></tr><tr><td>xi)</td><td>Earnings Revisions Signal: profits from revisions in analysts&#x27; earnings estimates. Even small changes in expectations can have a big impact on a stock&#x27;s price</td></tr></table>

## What our US (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Communication Services

John Hodulik on Walt Disney: We have a Buy rating on Disney. EPS estimates are starting to move up. The key is profitability in its Streaming segment, where it should achieve breakeven later this year. We estimate over time it can achieve NFLX-like levels in the mid-20s. We expect the Parks segment to continue to perform while Linear sports and entertainment contend with secular trends in TV.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Communication Services

John Hodulik on Paramount: We have a Sell rating given trends in US TV Advertising and accelerating cord cutting. We expect the company’s upcoming renewal with Charter to result in the loss of carriage for a number of networks, putting further pressure on linear TV trends. The company has made significant strides to cut losses in streaming (which we expect to continue) but believe the ultimate path to profitability is unclear. We expect the company to have just slightly positive FCF in 2024 as it benefits from political advertising and the Super Bowl but expect losses to return next year. Lastly, the company is subject to continued takeover speculation in the press and we do believe a deal is likely given external factors within the ownership of the company and the need for scale in the industry.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Jay Sole on Ralph Lauren Corp: We have a Buy rating on RL. We think the market doesn't appreciate the transformational changes RL has made to its brand image, distribution model, and cost structure. We forecast a 12% 5-yr. EPS CAGR and believe the stock looks inexpensive at the current valuation (link).

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Staples

Peter Grom on Brown Forman Corp: We are Neutral on the stock. BFB shares have been under pressure over the last 12 months driven by concerns that spirits category demand has/will continue to remain challenged. Should this result in greater promotional activity this could limit margin expansion as well, which has been a key tenet of the bull case. While these concerns are certainly fair, they also appear largely priced in with shares trading below historical averages.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Staples

Faham Baig on Altria Group Inc: We have a Sell rating on Altria. The group is facing headwinds from a more challenged US market (100% of EBIT), where it is investing behind lower-priced Marlboro Black, and should result in Smokeable sales and OCI declining in FY24. The company is also attempting to catch up in next-generation products (NGP), which will prove difficult, in our view, resulting in Altria's share of the nicotine category declining

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Financials

Timothy Chiodo on PayPal Holdings Inc: We are Neutral on Paypal. While we believe certain aspects of its innovation plans (e.g., lead-gen related revenue streams) have the potential to help be supportive in 2025 and beyond, there remains meaningful gross profit pressures facing the company over the next \~12-24 months (Branded Checkout gross profit pressures [increasing enterprise mix alongside transaction expenses based on volumes, which typically results in Branded GP growth a few hundred basis points below volume growth, UBSe], ongoing headwinds from the migration of legacy businesses [PayPal Pro] and product overhaul to Invoices, and OVAS [both credit and interest earned on customer funds]).

Alex Kramm on Factset Research Systems: We have a Neutral rating on Factset. While multi-year investments seem to have driven the company to a higher sustainable growth rate, a tougher end market environment has increased uncertainty around near- and medium-term targets again. Management recently lowered its FY24 guidance, but it is too early to know if the outlook has been fully de-risked.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Kevin Caliendo on Cardinal Health Inc: We have a Buy rating on the stock. The company has recently completed a long-term strategic review and has realigned assets to focus on its growth businesses, pharma distribution and home care services, while its acute care medical business continues its turnaround. EBIT growth has accelerated thanks to GLP-1s and better mix. An upcoming RFP with its second largest customer, United Heathcare, has created a near-term overhang in the stock.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Kevin Caliendo on Henry Schein Inc: We have a Neutral rating on HSIC. The company is a diversified dental and medical products distributor. Consumer spending patterns are negatively impacting demand for higher-cost dental procedures, including high-margin implants, and pricing of higher-end equipment is under competitive pressure, potentially impacting revenue growth for the industry. Also, the company is emerging from a broad cyber attack in 4Q23, the long-term impact to market share and customer mix is still an unknown.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Steven Fisher on Paccar Inc: We are Neutral on the stock. We expect a truck market downturn in 2024, which should be balanced by PCAR’s relative stability (strong balance sheet, low decrementals) and continued expansion in the parts business. Good execution, and visibility to solid production for the next few quarters should support earnings and limit downside. The bear case is that orders fall significantly over the next few months, driving downside for 2024, and that TPO margins fall back notably below 18%. The bull case is that freight activity reaccelerates, and freight rates start to improve in 2024, and PCAR earnings could see a strong upcycle starting in late 2024.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Kevin McVeigh on Paychex Ind: We are Neutral on Paychex because 1) durable growth + FCF generation continue to be constructive long-term despite macro uncertainty as we believe stock isn’t discounting enough macro concern, 2) stock has been option for yield investors given durable FCF, leading margins, amid \~3% dividend yield, 3) Paychex is more levered to SMBs, which could be challenging in 2024 as it anniversaries downmarket hiring which absorbed the slack upmarket, and 4) \$120 PT PAYX is 23.9x 2025E EPS ex-cash—compared to 3-yr range of 21.8x-35.2x + average of 28.5x [including cash]. Price Target based on 2 year multiple on CY25 estimates, rolled forward by Cost of Equity to derive 1 year Price Target.

Gavin Parsons on Howmet Aerospace Inc: We are Neutral on the stock. HWM is well positioned to capitalize on the recovery in large commercial aircraft production rates, but UBS sees limited upside to consensus estimates and valuation at the high-end of the historical range.

Gavin Parsons on RTX Corp: We are Neutral on RTX. Aerospace and Defense end markets are driving growth across the business, but RTX continues to face supply chain and quality issues that impact margins and cash flow. The latest Geared TurboFan engine appears contained but will remain a risk for the next 1-2 years in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Materials

Curt Woodworth on Nucor Corp: We have a Buy rating on Nucor owing to leverage from infrastructure tailwinds and strong organic growth profile.

Joshua Spector on PPG Industries: We have a Buy rating on PPG. We maintain a positive view as we see PPG benefitting from continued price/cost tailwinds, as well as potential volume recovery over time.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Materials

Joshua Spector on FMC Corp: We are Neutral on the stock. We maintain a more cautious view on FMC as we believe FMC may have less visibility on longer term crop chemical volumes, which is a risk to estimates over the next 2 years. This is in addition to risk of lower pricing

Joshua Spector on Air Products & Chemicals Inc: We are Neutral on the stock. We maintain a more cautious view of APD near term as we see a more challenging margin environment in Asia which could impact earnings growth over the next year. Also, longer term project timelines have been delayed, which means the market likely needs to wait longer for any contracts/de-risking events.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Michael Goldsmith on Avalonbay Communities: We have a Neutral rating on AvalonBay. The company’s Coastal portfolio should continue to deliver same store growth in the top end of the peer group given favorable supply/demand dynamics. Normalizing bad debt should be a tailwind for AVB in 2024. Additionally, we expect strong value creation from the development pipeline.

Michael Goldsmith on Simon Property Group: We have a Neutral rating on Simon Property Group. Underlying demand for quality mall and outlet space remains solid and SPG has visibility into continued leasing into 2024. Though, this will be tested in a slowing macro environment that could pressure discretionary spending. While the core business continues to outperform, debt refinancing and volatility from other platform investments could weigh on its ability to translate the fundamentals into earnings growth.

Alex Kramm on CBRE Group Inc: We have a Neutral rating on CBRE. We view the company as the blue chip in the CRE brokerage space. The company has diversified well into more recurring outsourcing businesses, which should have stable and attractive growth profiles. From a cyclical perspective, the stock should benefit from a thawing of capital markets activity and a more active leasing market.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Michael Goldsmith on Mid America Apartment: We have a Sell rating on MAA. We expect demand to hold up for Sunbelt Apartment properties, but we see supply pressure limiting pricing power through 2025. Deliveries in 2024 for the US should be 500k units followed by 460k in 2025 with a higher concentrating in the Sunbelt. The pre-COVID average was 300k units per year.

Michael Goldsmith on UDR Inc: We have a Neutral rating on UDR. While we expect UDR’s Coastal properties to perform relatively well, we expect significant pressure on its Sunbelt properties which comprise 25% of SSNOI. Additionally, unlike other Coastal Apartment REITs, UDR does not have a tailwind in 2024 from bad debt.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

Ross Fowler on Constellation Energy: We have a Buy rating on Constellation. We see CEG as the best positioned name within Power having, post the IRA, secured the nuclear production tax credit which provides a floor price of \$40 -- \$43.75/MWh on generation output from the nuclear fleet. The PTC value adjusts for inflation each year, and any price above \$40 -- \$43.75/MWh is additional margin to our forecast. We expect management to provide an update on free cash flow allocation that will either be a repurchase of shares or a consolidation of some of the merchant nuclear plants in the US that they do not currently own. We believe CEG should be valued at an infrastructure multiple with an 8% discount applied for nuclear operational risk or 10.8x 2024E EV/EBITDA.

Ross Fowler on CMS Energy Corp: We have a Buy rating on the stock. We see CMS as a quality utility with a solid balance sheet and a management team that has a long track record of delivering or exceeding initial earnings guidance for over a decade. We rank Michigan as a Tier 2 (of 5) regulator and rank CMS in our top quartile for both regulatory quality and earnings per share growth

Gregg Orrill on Edison International: We have a Buy rating on EIX. We see the company as undervalued to a continued constructive view of California regulation as evidenced by the increase in the cost of capital in December for 2024. Catalysts upcoming include the guidance update on the 2/22 earnings call, potential approval of a wireless lease plan and proceeds from a catastrophic event memo account. California’s commitment to clean energy supports high single digit rate base growth and recovery of legacy wildfire claims in 2025 represents a \$2.4 billion recovery opportunity,

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

Ross Fowler on Pinnacle West Capital: We have a Sell rating on PNW. At current valuations we see risks to PNW around the next general rate case in Arizona where we rate that state a Tier 5 jurisdiction and the company as fourth quartile in terms of regulatory quality. We also see significant continued regulatory lag for the company given the growth in their service territory. We expect a decision in the current rate case process in Arizona on 2/22; regulators in Arizona are scheduled to vote on the final order in the Arizona Public Service rate case. The company is seeking a \$378mm net revenue increase, the ACC Staff supported \$282mm and the Recommended Order and Opinion, after a negative adjustment, supports \$226mm. Beyond the revenue and the ROE, the key item we’ll be watching for is approval of the System Reliability Rider, which would allow for rate recovery of new generation assets placed into service in between rate cases.

Ross Fowler on Dominion Energy: We are Neutral on Dominion. Continued focus remains on the strategic review for the company, the last element of which is a potential sale of a stake in the regulated offshore wind project in Virginia. Once that is complete we expect the company to host an analyst day, although there still remains significant uncertainty in the market around 2025 earnings power (UBSe/Cons \$3.45). Long term risks remain around Virginia regulation in the next biennial, execution risks of offshore wind construction, any contingencies potentially kept around OSW, and management execution. Upsides remain around the OSW sale and completion of the strategic review revaluing shares.

Ross Fowler on Eversource Energy: We have a Neutral rating on Eversource. ES sold their stake in the South Fork and Revolution offshore wind projects to GIP, but risks remain around the kept contingencies on cost overruns. Upside could be driven by potential to resolve the Sunrise wind project and be out of offshore wind. Risks remain around Connecticut and FERC transmission regulation, cost contingencies and management execution.

## What our European (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Ondrej Cabejsek on Telenor Asa: We have a Buy rating on Telenor. The company has derisked and simplified its business in Asia significantly and the focus is returning to the healthy Nordic markets where Telenor is now growing EBITDA by MSD with good visibility on both the top line as well as various cost levers (energy, copper shutdown). Its capex/sales is falling from 19% in 2022 to 14% in 2025E as FTTH/5G rollouts are being completed. FCF from Asia is set to double on the back of synergies from mergers in Thailand/Malaysia between 2022 and 2025. As a result of all of the above, we estimate Telenor’s FCF is going from cNOK8bn in 2023 to cNOK14bn (c9% yield) by 2025E. Telenor is already paying one of the highest dividends in the sector at c8.5% and we believe this is sustainable and growing, supported by c2.2x ND/EBITDA.

Adam Berlin on Publicis Groupe: We have a Buy rating on Publicis. Our 2025E EPS is 4% ahead of consensus. While a buyback of above €200m would have been an incremental positive, we expect M&A to help Publicis deliver an 8% 2024-26E EPS CAGR. Along with the larger dividend, this implies an annual TSR of 12% even if shares do not continue to re-rate. But we think a further re-rating is still possible with shares trading at 12x.

Adam Berlin on Universal Music Group: We have a Buy rating on UMG. We believe a combination of momentum in paid streaming (penetration and price rises), cost savings and limited further spend on catalogue acquisitions means UMG can deliver strong FCF growth to >€2bn by 2025E, and 11% ahead of consensus. In 2024E, we forecast 12% growth in UMG organic streaming revenues (vs VA cons 11%). On our estimates the group trades on an attractive 4.1% FY25 FCF yield

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Adam Berlin on Ubisoft Entertainment: We have a Sell rating on Ubisoft. Visibility is poor, execution is a major risk based on past performance, while UBSe FCF is only forecast to turn positive in FY28 with Ubisoft only generating positive FCF in 5 of the last 10 yrs.

Adam Berlin on ITV Plc: We are Neutral on ITV. While we see value in ITV underpinned by Studios, near-term catalysts are limited with sentiment impaired by scepticism around ITV's ability to scale EBITA from FY23 lows and achieve c67% of Group revenues from Digital (c£750m) and Studios in FY26. On c6.7x FY25E PE, the Group trades in-line with the sector and we see further downside risk stemming from a weaker than expected UK TV ad market, streamers with new ad funded models taking revenue share and difficulty in mitigating cost inflation after a number of years of cost out.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Chris Huang on Brunello Cucinelli Spa: We have a Buy rating on Brunello. We believe 2024 will once again showcase BC's superior fundamentals, highlighted by the overall challenging macro and slowing sector context. We expect the company to deliver +11% organic sales growth in 2024 and continue its +MSD% outperformance vs. the sector, thanks to the brand's absolute luxury positioning which entails strong pricing power and resilient volume growth. This combined with more meaningful operating leverage from 2024 onwards as BC exceeds the €1bn sales mark, drive our EPS estimates \~MSD% above cons with +low-teens% 2023-26 CAGR. (for more, see Brunello Cucinelli S.p.A “2024 Outlook: Weaving exquisite yet resilient growth)

Michal Potyra on LPP SA: We have a Buy rating on LPP. The stock offers high beta exposure to recovering consumption in the CEE region. We expect top line and margins recovery to progress throughout 2024.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Yashraj Rajani on Zalando: We think Zalando remains the best-in-class Online pure play fashion player in Europe, but we think expectations for 2024 and 2025 both on GMV growth and adj. EBIT margins remain full. Recent Textilwirtschaft data and Germany Retail sales suggest some weakness in Nov/Dec sales. This along with soft consumer sentiment in Germany keeps us cautious. With limited catalysts for returning Online penetration and ZAL’s topline growth, we remain Neutral.

Saranja Sivachelvam on Kingfisher Plc: It is our top Sell rating, because 1) Sales: top three markets are in structural decline, 2) Profit: consensus and many bulls on the buy side expect KGF to return to peak margins, which it hasn’t been able to do despite all the self help in the past two years, and 3) FCF: company guidance for a FCF of £500-600m is too punchy in the future, in our view, given that KGF has never accomplished this before and we don't see a catalyst beyond this year (inventory reversal) to suggest that it can do so.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Staples

Guillaume Delmas on Danone: We have a Buy rating on Danone. We believe that 2024 will be the year during which Danone's progress under its "Renew" strategy will become clearly visible. Fast improving volume/ mix and relatively resilient pricing should provide tangible evidence of the group's structurally enhanced portfolio. We believe further increase in the company's reinvestment efforts will provide the fuel for sustainable, mixled and gross margin enhancing top-line outperformance. This should enable Danone to consistently report top quartile organic EBIT growth in the high single digit percentage range over the coming years. Plus, we believe several optionalities could potentially result in Danone's shares overshooting our price target, namely a birth rate rebound in China, a positive impact arising from the widespread adoption of GLP-1 drugs in the US and signals of higher cash returns from 2025 onwards.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Anna Kishmariya on Orlen SA: We are Neutral on the stock. While Orlen’s extensive investment plan over the next \~5 years provides for a negative FCF yield on our assumptions, the group has low leverage (ND/EBITDA at 0.2x in 3Q23) and reiterated its commitment to the base dividend which should yield 6.1% for FY23 and 6.3% for FY24. The recent change in Polish government is broadly well-received by the market, especially for SOEs, but for a meaningful re-rating of Orlen shares, in our view, we would need to see an update on the group’s strategy or projects planned which is yet to be announced.

Henri Patricot on Repsol SA: We rate Repsol Buy, as we see the company as likely to surprise positively on cashflow generation which should allow it to maintain high shareholder returns and transition towards low-carbon faster than peers. We expect the company to continue raising the dividends and maintain buybacks at \~€1bn p.a., providing a sector-leading distribution yield of 13% over 2024-25.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Joshua Stone on OMV AG: For OMV, the petrochemical market remains weak, negotiations on a potential merger in the Middle-East are taking longer than expected, and the stock no longer stands out to us on valuation with a 2024E FCF yield of 11%, in-line with the peer group. We are Neutral with a PT of €40/sh.

Henri Patricot on Equinor Asa: We rate Equinor Sell as we expect lower European gas prices to drive lower shareholder returns. We see downside risk to consensus expectations for this year as we expect lower European gas prices to drive earnings sharply lower. We are 2% below consensus for 2024E EPS and the forward curves for oil and gas prices imply another 21% downside to our forecasts.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Nasib Ahmed on Aviva Plc: Buy rated and most preferred life insurance focused name in the UK given high capital return (inc. buybacks) and diversification. Aviva is the only composite insurer in the UK which allows it to grow in markets where the cycle results in the greatest ROC with capital diversification on top.

Michal Potyra on Powszechny Zaklad Ubezpiecze: We have a Neutral rating on PZU. The stock offers a lower beta exposure to macro recovery in Poland, benefiting from recovering motor market pricing, life segment margins and high bond yields. Valuation (when adjusted for PZU stakes in banks) is in line with historical average levels.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Justin Forsythe on Worldline SA: We have a Sell rating on Worldline. We think numbers for 2024 are not fully derisked, and on a cash flow basis the valuation appears more reasonable. Also we would see a full fledged take out as upside risk, however there could be a French solution / minority stake / backing by existing partners, less likely to result in M&A related upside compared to Nexi in our view. Upside case is they just hit numbers and operational risks don’t materialise with 200m restructuring plans.

Sanjena Dadawala on Close Brothers Group: We are Neutral on the stock. Close Brothers faces uncertainty regarding the outcome and potential financial impact of the FCA's review of historical motor finance commission arrangements. Given sensitivity to several assumptions, a wide range of potential customer redress outcomes are possible (UBSe £30-830m), with £280m (280bps of capital) reflecting the mid-point of key assumptions and what’s included in our forecasts. The firm recently announced it won't pay dividends for FY24 to build capital strength and other potential options to bolster capital include slowing lending, significant risk transfer securitisation (both of which would reduce RWAs but also mean lower profits), raising equity and asset sales. Despite a stock which is down 60% since the FCA’s announcement, we remain Neutral rated given the uncertainty which is expected to remain for some time.

Johan Ekblom on KBC Group NV: We have a Neutral rating on KBC. The company remains at a significant premium to peers, both in the Benelux and in the broader European bank sector. We see downside risks to consensus medium term projections, predominantly on the back of higher expenses and to lesser extent from lower revenue growth. KBC remains one of our least preferred European banks at current levels.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Health Care

Graham Doyle on Convatec Group: We have a Buy rating on Convatec. A turnaround story where revenue growth has been accelerating and now margins are improving driven by a better business mix. The launch of new products should sustain this into the mid 2020s.

Xian Deng on Ipsen: We have a Buy rating on Ipsen. It is a midcap pharma company that grows at above sector rate but trades at a discount. Ipsen is one of the least binary stocks in our SMID pharma/biotech coverage as the growth is largely driven by approved assets. We like Dysport (Ipsen’s botox equivalent for cosmetics use), and we believe its consumer characteristics could drive sustained long term growth. The longevity is often underappreciated and it is what makes Dysport the biggest asset by NPV in our model.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Rory McKenzie on Spie SA: We have a Buy rating on SPIE, a defensive compounder with exposure to sustainability-driven themes, trading at an attractive valuation. Recent results showed organic growth is still tracking ahead of expectations, and we see a c5% organic CAGR achievable over the mid-term given the majority of revenues are generated from spending on e.g. improving building efficiency, the energy transition, and connectivity. With scope for continued bolt-on M&A in fragmented markets, we therefore see a potential 8-9% adj net income CAGR that looks undervalued today on c11x EV/EBITA..

Cristian Nedelcu on DHL Group: We have a Buy rating on DHL. The company is one of the main beneficiaries in European transport from a restocking cycle. Express B2B volumes/global air freight volumes are depressed (on a 12m rolling basis stand at \~95% of 2019 levels). An acceleration in volumes in the context of the higher operating leverage of the company would be supportive for earnings upgrades as well as re-rating. While forecasting the exact timing of a restocking remains difficult, we believe it could be a theme for 2H24 or early 2025.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Nicole Manion on Rentokil Initial Plc: We are Sell-rated on Rentokil. We believe the company has a bigger growth issue in US pest control than is appreciated by the market, and see significant consensus earnings risks ahead because of the investments need to address this while integrating Terminix. While we see no reason why RTO can't ultimately deliver on back office synergy targets for TMX, we are concerned that consensus expectations over the next couple of years do not: i) allow for sufficient reinvestment to address the growth gap; and ii) appreciate the related headwinds to cash generation and earnings quality.

Marcus Cole on Travis Perkins Plc: We are Neutral on Travis. We think prospects remain challenging for the company as a result of: (1) pricing pressure; which may have only just started, (2) Toolstation France continues to be a drag on profits, (3) private peers appear to be aggressive on taking market share, and (4) leverage limits balance sheet optionality (FY23E: 2.5x).

Sebastian Vogel on Belimo Holdings AG: We have a Sell rating on Belimo. We expect the company’s skew on the non-residential construction market to burden especially its FY24E’s European business. Moreover, we see the latest FX developments adding further downward pressure on the group’s profitability, in light of the pronounced transactional risk which the company operates. We don’t see these downside risks being properly reflected in its still demanding valuation.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Michael Briest on Capgemini SE: We have a Buy rating on the stock. In the highly fragmented market for IT Services, Capgemini is a global, scale player. It can adapt its skills base rapidly given its high offshore footprint, which also supports a competitive margin profile.

Michael Briest on Computacenter: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Computacenter’s exposure to large, global enterprises and public sector clients provides macro diversification. Major cloud and internet customers in the US provide good growth prospects in Technology Sourcing, while Managed Services provides customer stickiness in Europe.

Michael Briest on Bechtle AG: We are Neutral on the stock. Bechtle is the largest IT system house in Germany and is increasing its presence in Europe through acquisitions. Q4 showed a re-acceleration to 8% growth led by public sector and major accounts. Cash flow is strong.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Harry Blaiklock on Nordic Semiconductor: We recently upgraded the stock to Neutral. Visibility is still limited given elevated channel inventories but it appears end-demand is finally bottoming out. The shape and speed of recovery still remains uncertain.

Francois-Xavier Bouvignies on Nokia (rated Neutral) /Ericsson (rated Sell): We believe the current macro investment will continue to weigh on telco capex spending and therefore impact Nokia and Ericsson revenues in 2024.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Geoff Haire on Air Liquide SA: We have a Buy rating on Air Liquide. We believe the company offers investors hight single digit earnings growth going forward as well as have a critical role on the emerging low-carbon hydrogen market. The shares are trading at a wide valuation discount to its closest peer Linde.

Geoff Haire on Arkema: We rate Arkema Buy. In our view, Arkema should benefit from volume recovery in 2024 as end- market inventories appear to be close to average levels; this should drive a 13% y/y recovery in EBITDA in 2024, in line with the EU Diversified peers

Gregor Kuglitsch on Wienerberger: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Wienerberger has big volume recovery potential plus it looks cheap but there is risk that they get hit by a lower gas price (they were hedged when it went up).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Priyanka Patel on K+S AG: We are Sell rated on K+S (PT 12). The higher nitrogen fertiliser prices are negative for potash demand as it's more discretionary for farmers, and we’ve already seen potash prices fall 14% ytd. We are also bearish on the longer term supply outlook for potash, with BHP planning to add c. 10% of current supply to the market by the end of the decade likely to push the market into oversupply and pose significant downside earnings risk for K+S given its position at the high end of the cost curve. K+S reports 4Q23 results on 14th March, and investors will likely be focused on 2024 EBITDA guidance; we are currently 5% below cons for 2024, and see more downside risk as potash prices continue to fall.

Daniel Major on Boliden AB: We are Neutral on the stock. BOL has exposure to commodities with robust fundamentals (copper/zinc) but operational issues/grades in mines and lower TC’s and the impact of a fire at the Ronnskar smelter are likely to persist in 2024, suppressing earnings, and high capex will weigh on FCF.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Charles Boissier on Unibail Rodamco Westfield: The research team has a Neutral given the strong share price performance. The company’s recent FY23 results marked a reinstatement of the dividend distribution—following 3 years of suspension, as a result of the impact from Covid-19. Key metrics (footfall, retail sales, etc.) are now above prepandemic levels in Europe, the company being confident that the prospects are positive for its portfolio of prime dominant malls going forward.

Charles Boissier on Warehuoses de Pauw: The research team has a Buy rating on WDP. European logistics continues to enjoy a number of structural tailwinds (i.e. near-shoring, e-commerce, etc.), from which WDP is benefitting through robust operations and stabilising valuations as demonstrated at its recent FY23 results.

Charles Boissier on Merlin Properties: The research team has a Buy rating on Merlin. Occupancy has just reached an all-time high as Spain’s largest real estate company continues to enjoy strong indexation and better-than-European average economic growth, with future portfolio expansion likely coming from logistics and data center, where fundamentals are strong and management is not ruling out a potential strategic partnership.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Tommaso Operto on Swiss Prime Site: We have a Neutral rating on SPS. The company is an office-led Swiss company with a quickly growing asset management business. The stock offers an attractive dividend yield of 4.0%, but the repositioning of Jelmoli will require elevated capex, and an already relatively high LTV ratio of just shy of 40% will limit further upside potential on the payout, we believe.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Gonzalo Sanchez-Bordona on Enel Spa: We have a Buy rating on Enel. We liked the plan presented in the recent CMD, with focus on cash generation, cost reduction, capex cuts and potential dividend upside. We see the company delivering higher than expected earnings growth through 2026 and with >8bn lower net capex than in the previous plan. Stock continues trading at an unjustified discount in light of superior dividend, attractive growth and balance sheet derisking in our view.

Wanda Serwinowska on E.ON: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Given macro uncertainty into 2024, we believe e.on represents a good value proposition given: (i) lower risk (75% EBITDA is regulated), (ii) strong balance sheet relative to peers, and (iii) the highest 2023-27E DPS growth across regulated peers. The stock trades at a discount to its peers and any move to risk-off or rate-cutting scenarios in 2024 should add further potential upside, in our view

Wanda Serwinowska on Engie: We have a Buy rating on Engie. In our view, the reasons for Engie's historically low multiples are being addressed systematically, with: (i) volatile commodity-sensitive activities disposed; (ii) opaque energy services activities disposed; (iii) a nuclear exit pathway agreed in Belgium, with liabilities capped at last; (iv) a company-wide cost & simplification programme under way; (v) new multi-year earnings and dividend guidance issued; and (vi) a management focus on consistent delivery. The result should be a more stable and predictable business that no longer deserves the current discount to utility sector multiples, but track records take time to be established and this is likely to be a multi-year process.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Mark Freshney on Severn Trend Plc: We initiated coverage with a Sell rating and 2,500p price target. We see Severn Trent as having the most fully priced shares in the regulated utility sector. The equity capital raise makes the dividend sustainable, but given it was priced at close to 1.0x RAB, it dilutes upside. We do not expect the advantage on outcome delivery incentives (extra returns for meeting targets) to be sustainable in the long term. SVT is the most expensive UK water stock in our coverage.

## What our Chinese (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Consumer Discretionary

Kenneth Fong on PDD Holdings (PDD UW): We have a Buy rating on PDD. Strong domestic operation with focus on low ASP product that is defensive amid economic downcycle in China. We also like the upside from its overseas expansion from Temu that is not completely in the price in our view.

Paul Gong on Great Wall Motor (2333 HK): We have a Buy rating on Great Wall Motor.
Premiumization, electrification and export growth continue to drive earnings growth.

Samuel Wang on Haidilao International (6862 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. We expect HDL to record strong table turnover growth yoy in 1H24E benefiting from product innovation and marketing efforts. We also expect resumption of store expansion starting from this year.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Consumer Discretionary

Paul Gong on Dongfeng Motor Group (489 HK): We are Neutral on Dongfeng because of its declining profit amid market share loss

Paul Gong on Nio Inc (NIO UN): We have a Neutral rating on Nio as the company is facing fiercer than ever competition.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Staples

Henry Liu on Alibaba Health information (241 HK): We are Neutral on Ali Health. We are concerned on the expected growth deceleration in its FY24 (due to high base in FY23). But this is a high-beta stock, so if sentiment in Hang Seng Tech improves, it could see some good rallying.

Christine Peng on China Resources Beer Holding (291 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. We remain concerned about its newly acquired Baijiu business but also believe investors have no earnings expectations for this business. Our DCF-based price target of HK\$41.23 (WACC: 7.9%) implies 21x/18x 2024E/2025E PE on a 13% 2023-25E EPS CAGR driven by expanded volume at its premium beer segment.

Christine Peng on Tingyi Holdings Co (322 HK): A steady or lower sugar price in 2024 could be a positive factor for Tingyi's earnings growth, in our view, along with a continuously upgrading instant noodle market. Our DCF-based price target of HK \$11.76 (WACC: 7.1%) implies 16x/15x 2024E/2025E PE on a 9% 2023-25E EPS CAGR. We maintain our Buy rating and believe Tingyi's 100% dividend payout will provide valuation support.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Financials

May Yan on Agricultural Bank of China (1288 HK): We recently upgraded ABC-H from Neutral to Buy. While we lowered our forecast for ABC’s 2024E earnings growth from 5.2% to 2.7% due to major NIM headwinds that weigh on the overall industry, we still expect ABC to outperform its peers, supported by its strong loan growth owing to its competitive advantages in rural- and county-area banking, sufficient provision buffer, as well as strong incentives to replenish its core tier-1 capital. We slightly revised down the CoE from 16.6% to 15.5% to showcase ABC’s better earnings resilience in the economic downcycle. Under our three-stage DDM model, our price target for ABC-H was raised from HK\$3.10 to HK\$3.45. The implied P/BV multiple would be 0.50x (vs historical average at 0.58x).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Financials

Haifeng Cao on Haitong Securities Co Ltd (6837 HK): We have a Neutral rating on Haitong. It is more exposed to H share market (HSI index down 14% in 2023 vs Wind All A share index down 5%) - so we believe its 2023 earnings are likely to be weaker than other leading brokers.

Charles Zhou on ZhongAn Online (6060 HK): We are Neutral on the stock because 1) regulatory risk appears to be non-negligible. Online insurance is still at a nascent stage, and there are potentially regulatory gaps to be filled, such as marketing tactics and commission arrangement, and 2) ZhongAn’s business model heavily relies on its ecosystem partners. While the insurer is ramping up its proprietary channel, it takes time to achieve a balanced channel mix.

Charles Zhou on New China Life Insurance (1336 HK): We are Neutral on NCI because 1) it has a relatively high value of new business (VNB) and embedded value (EV) sensitivity to investment return. The equity market and interest rate environment in China are still subject to uncertainty, 2) agency productivity is low relative to listed peers, meaning that its growth is more headcount-driven. We highlight the huge recruitment model has become less effective, due to the diminishing demographic dividend, and 3) business quality is not ideal vs peers, as evidenced by its relatively low persistency ratio. NCI just tuned down its operating assumptions last year.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Health Care

Chen Chen on Akeso Inc (9926 HK): We have a Buy rating on Akeso because 1) AK112 lung cancer (PD-L1 positive, head-to-head Keytruda) data readout in 2Q24 and approval in China in 3Q24; 2) approval of PCSK9 and IL12/23 in the next 6-12 months; 3) product sales ramp-up.

Anita Wei on Sinopharm Group (1099 HK): We have a Buy rating on Sinopharm. It is the No.1 player in pharma distribution industry in China, and we like it due to the defensive business nature. Medium preference on Sinopharm, investor feedback was generally quite constructive and accept the value stock theme. Catalysts for Sinopharm include: 1) recovery of hospital-end business after anti-corruption campaign; 2) quarterly results vs. the guidance of 3-5% above industry growth.

Jun Deng on Hansoh Pharmaceutical Group (3692 HK): We have a Buy rating on Hansoh. For the commercialization of in-market products, we expect company to continue to maximise revenue potential of its 8 novel drugs in China this year. Particularly, its core drug Aumolertinib could potentially deliver robust revenue growth on the back of 1) market share gain in 1L setting and 2) stable price throughout 2024. For the R&D of pipeline drugs, we expect catalysts in multiple fronts including: 1) potential NDA approval in China for Ibrexafungerp (VVC); 2) NDA/ marketing application filings in China for Aumolertinib (adjuvant setting) and potentially KiOmedinevsOne (knee osteoarthritis); 3) ph3 trial initiation for GLP-1/GIP in China, potential initiation of pivotal trial for B7-H3 ADC in China, ph1 trial initiation for B7-H3 ADC and B7-H4 ADC outside China by GSK; 4) multiple ADC programs to enter clinical stage. For business development, we expect potential out-license and in-license opportunities.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Health Care

Chen Chen on Hangzhou Tigermed Consulting (3347 HK): We are Neutral on the stock. Uncertainties in China's healthcare funding and macro environment have obscured the visibility of Tigermed's near-term growth.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Industrials

Wei Shen on Weichai Power (2338 HK): We have a Buy rating on the Weichai Power. It is beneficiary from China’s heavy duty truck upcycle from 2023. The company is also gaining market share due to its dominance in natural gas engine segment. Its investment in agriculture machinery, big bore engine and automated warehouse starts to bear fruit as well.

Robin Xu on Zhejiang Expressway (576 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. We see market concerns on 2023 DPS decline post the rights issue being overdone, as we expect higher dividend payout and earnings growth in 2023 should support 2023 DPS to be higher than 2022. And the upcoming 2023 result in end March together with DPS announcement, will likely be the near term catalyst.

Perry Yeung on Boc Aviation (2588 HK): We have a Buy rating on BOC. We expect a recovery in net lease yield in 2024 and beyond. While the high interest rate environment in the near term could continue to present upward pressure on the cost of debt, UBS's US economics team forecasts significantly lower US interest rates with a 250bp cut in the Fed Fund rate in 2024E. At the same time, robust airline earnings should support fleet expansion, while the slow delivery rate of new aircraft could prompt airlines to source new aircraft from lessors, translating to stronger leasing demand and lease rate.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Info Tech

Randy Abrams on Hua Hong Semiconductor (1347 HK): We recently upgraded its rating to Neutral on a view the stock was near trough support on P/B and also its utilization rate finally bottoming to help profitability also bottom, though we’re still acknowledging they face more competition from China’s aggressive mature capacity build-out and still only mild recovery off the bottom so may still have a muted sales/margin outlook through the recovery.

Grace Chen on AAC Technologies Holdings (2018 HK): We have a Sell rating on AAC. The company reported challenging H123 performance, which supports our cautious view on the company given its high exposure to smartphones. AAC expected a recovery in H223 with smartphone demand outlook stabilizing. However, we believe it will take time before its diversification efforts bear fruit. In addition, its handset lens peer – Sunny Optical – had a profit warning for H223 results in January.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Materials

Xinlei Li on cement names: We expect China cement utilization to continue to slide 1- 2ppt in 2024-25E due to overcapacity situation since: 1) China cement sector face structural demand downside risks from property downcycle and slowing infrastructure investments; and 2) Surprisingly, cement sector had another capex expansion cycle right after China's supply reform in 2016-17 with capex up 49% on average during 2018-23 vs. 2011-15, despite peaking demand. Among cement coverage, we least favor South China cement names like CR given the most supply pressures.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Real Estate

John Lam on China Resources Land (1109 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock because 1) The company has a higher than peers land acquisition intensity (land acquisition / contract sales) at 55% in 2023, resulting in stronger than peers growth in saleable resources for 2024. 2) The company’s 42% of earnings is generated from recurring business (ie rental income, property management business) in 1H23, and looking ahead in the next 3-5 years, we expect the % of earnings generated from recurring business is going to increase and will reach over 50%, which should deserve a re-rating.

John Lam on China Overseas Land (688 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock because 1) The company has a higher than peers land acquisition intensity (land acquisition / contract sales) at 47% in 2023, resulting in stronger than peers growth in saleable resources for 2024. 2) The company is likely to benefit from the government’s urban village renovation program, as the company is more exposed to the tier 1-2 cities, where the program is conducted.

John Lam on China Resources Mixc Lifesty (1209 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock because 1) Being the largest luxury mall manager in China, the company benefits from luxury brands store opening in tier 2 cities, where it remains under-penetrated. The high entry barrier of managing a luxury mall suggests they are likely to be the beneficiary. 2) The company also benefits from the potential shopping malls C-REITs issuance, which improve the shopping mall liquidity , and hence higher demand for mall manager.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Real Estate

John Lam on China Jinmao Holdings Group (817 HK): We are Neutral on Jinmao because 1) The weak new home sales (-36% YoY for top 100 developers contract sales in Jan 2024) is likely to cause cash outflow for Jinmao. 2) Lack of land acquisition in 2023 caused the company to lack new saleable resources for 2024 and hence contract sales growth in 2024. 3) Property price decline since 2H23 is likely to cause margin compression

John Lam on China Vanke Co Ltd (2202 HK): We are Neutral on Vanke because 1) The weak new home sales (-36% YoY for top 100 developers contract sales in Jan 2024) is likely to cause cash outflow for Vanke. 2) A slow land acquisition in 2023 (land acquisition / contract sales at 23% vs SOE peers of 40-50%) caused the company to run out of new saleable resources for 2024 and hence a negative contract sales growth in 2024. 3) Property price decline since 2H23 is likely to cause margin compression

John Lam on Country Garden Services (6098 HK): We are Neutral on Country Garden Services because its sister company (Country Garden Holdings) has defaulted its USD bonds, and caused home buyers to refuse to buy their properties. Country Garden Holdings needs to complete their properties' construction, which is likely to need capital. Market is concerned about the cash flow leakage from Country Garden Services to Country Garden Holdings for project completion, as both companies are controlled by the same family, and the government is pushing Country Garden Holdings for project completion for social stability.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Utilities

Ken Liu on China Utilities names: Fundamentally these stocks are under pressure because 1) moderation of overall power demand in China due to sluggish economy 2) renewable power (wind solar) prices under more pressure than conventional (coal, nuclear, hydro, etc.) given oversupply in renewables in some regions, 3) subsidies delay on renewables 4) project IRR pressure on renewables. This makes China Resources Power (coal power + renewables) one of our most preferred names within the cohort.

Amily Guo on Kunlun Energy (135 HK): We have a Buy rating on Kunlun. The company maintained higher than national-average retail gas volume growth in 2023 with a stable dollar margin, and we expect it to maintain a higher than national average volume growth in 2024. Our PT of HK\$8.90 is derived from a three-stage DCF methodology, implying 11x 2023E PE and 3-4% 2023E dividend yield

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Utilities

Ken Liu on ENN Energy Holdings (2688 HK): We are Neutral on ENN as it suffers from 1) weaker gas volume due to sluggish economy, and 2) weaker earnings from connection fee due to sluggish property market. There could potentially be some upside to the current share price if new management could execute well, but we do think overall market conditions would likely give share price more pressure.

## What our Japanese (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Consumer Discretionary

Kohei Takahashi on Denso Corp: We have a Buy rating on Denso. It should be a beneficiary of US electrification with various powertrains e.g. HEV, PHEV, and BEV due to its wide range of products.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Kikkoman Corp: We have a Buy rating on Kikkoman because of 1) steady topline growth of soy sauce in US and EU even under recession, 2) guidance this year is still conservative, 3) raw material cost increase will calm down, so profitability will expand in overseas business even next year, and 4) high visibility for next year.

Faham Baig on Japan Tobacco Inc: We have a Buy rating on the stock. JT is on the front foot in combustibles - helped by peers seeking to transition and its exposure to relatively inelastic emerging markets (c50% of sales) - and an improved offering in heated tobacco should drive an acceleration in EBIT growth, in our view. With the group close to a net cash position, we expect JT to accelerate the dividend and announce a buyback, which would still provide optionality for M&A or MoF stake.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Meiji Holdings Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Meiji as 1) we do not see signs to recover topline of yogurt after price hiking in food business, 2) next year new factories in China will start to work even under recession, and 3) unclear visibility next year.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Financials

Taiki Okada on T&D Holdings Inc: We have a Buy rating on T&D because 1) increasing share in the niche life insurance market, 2) capital management improvement incl. shareholders’ return expansion, and 3) high sensitivity vs JGB long-end interest rate

Taiki Okada on MUFC: We have a Buy rating on MUFC because 1) overseas NII strength with US interest rate rise, 2) domestic loan and spread expansion, especially for large corporate. BoJ policy change is additional catalyst, and 3) yen weakness supports consolidated earnings

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Tomoko Yoshihara on Terumo Corp: We are Neutral on Terumo. The fundamentals should remain brisk in the next 6-9 months due to the cost reduction efforts and brisk market environment in US. The new management team could focus more on the restructuring of low margin business and the company has a good reputation on M&A for long-term growth.

Kasumi Haruta on Shionogi & Co Ltd: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Shionogi has strength in infectious diseases and they had solid earnings from HIV franchise royalty and COVID-19/Influenza treatment. Next catalyst will be readout for phase 2 data of S-309309 which is for obesity drug scheduled in Apr-Jun. If they could have success in the trial, they are highly likely to enter a partnership with pharmaceutical company for S-309309 and consider combination with GLP-1 agonist.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Kasumi Haruta on Eisai Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Eisai. Speed of ramp up for Alzheimer’s drug Leqembi is still slow and actual sales is likely to miss 4Q consensus. Company is scheduled to file SubQ formulation by 3/24 but the launch will be 3/25 at the earliest. Therefore Leqembi is likely to continue slow ramp up for 1-2 years from now. If market cannot find the inflection point, share price will likely start looking at patent cliff for mainstay product Lenvima. Share price should be under pressure until we see strong sales momentum.

Tomoko Yoshihara on M3 Inc: We are Neutral on M3. The core online marketing service for pharmaceutical companies remains sluggish for the next 6-9 months. We do not see other business to offset the weakness in online marketing service for pharmaceutical companies, there is some risk for conservative guidance for FY24 in Apr.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Industrials

Harunobu Goroh on Furukawa Electric Co Ltd: We are Neutral on the stock. In terms of the external environment, the recovery in auto-related fields is a positive factor, but prolonged inventory adjustment for communications solutions-related products is still a negative factor. The company has accelerated investment in its core businesses, including communications solutions and energy infrastructure, but has been affected by demand fluctuations, and has lagged behind peers in improving profitability.

## What our Australian (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Tim Piper on GUD Holdings Ltd: We have a Buy rating on GUD. Near-term earnings leveraged to supply recovery in key OEMs (Ford, Toyota, etc.) through APG business, Underlying Core Auto business largely non-discretionary and less exposed to consumer slowdown. Continued de-leveraging of the balance sheet over the next \~12 months may see further acquisition opportunities explored. Valuation undemanding at 13.9x P/ E.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Tim Piper on Bapcor Ltd: \~70%+ of group earnings generated from non-discretionary aftermarket auto trade channel, a rational industry with high barriers to entry. Both sellside and buy-side expectations associated with ‘Better than before’ transformation program conservative (<50% of targeted incremental FY25e EBIT benefit priced in), in our view, this program represents the vast majority of potential medium-term earnings upside and valuation looks attractive if management executes on targeted savings.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Shaun Cousins on Endeavour Group Ltd: We have a Buy rating on the stock. The EDV share price has fallen due to rising regulatory risk in its gaming business, LFL sales growth below its peer in Retail, and rising debt levels & borrowing costs. We have reviewed the regulatory risk in detail and identified mitigating factors for EDV, with the current share price incorporating a significant decline in gaming EBIT from FY27E onwards. We also see further support as shifting capex strategy and capital recycling improves Hotels ROFE.

Apoorv Sehgal on Graincorp Ltd: Buy based on through-the-cycle earnings and scope for capital returns. We expect a likely working capital unwind over FY24E (as crop conditions normalise in dryer conditions) to support a net core cash position expanding from \$200m at 1H23 to \$460m by FY24E, or 30% of mcap, before likely \~\$104m of proceeds from sale of its 8.5% UMG stake. We expect GNC to return a portion of this cash to shareholders. Stock trading on \~5x through-the-cycle EBITDA looks relatively cheap.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Health Care

Laura Sutcliffe on Telix Pharmaceuticals: We have a Buy rating on Telix. We believe the company has a de-risked late stage asset (kidney cancer diagnostic) that is currently outside the share price.

Laura Sutcliffe on CSL Ltd: We have a Buy rating on CSL. The core business in Ig is improving as expected and we see potential upside to our forecasts if gross margins reach pre-Covid levels at the shorter end of the company’s 3-5 year time horizon.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Health Care

Laura Sutcliffe on Cochlear Ltd: We have a Sell rating for the stock. Underlying dynamics are currently strong, but we see the risk profile for COH shares changing as a potential data readout in the vaccines world later in the year could mean a threat to the top line in the mid term via lower incidence of infant deafness.

KEY DEFINITIONS: Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Short-Term Ratings reflect the expected near-term (up to three months) performance of the stock and do not reflect any change in the fundamental view or investment case. Equity Price Targets have an investment horizon of 12 months.

EXCEPTIONS AND SPECIAL CASES: UK and European Investment Fund ratings and definitions are: Buy: Positive on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core Banding Exceptions (CBE): Exceptions to the standard +/-6% bands may be granted by the Investment Review Committee (IRC). Factors considered by the IRC include the stock's volatility and the credit spread of the respective company's debt. As a result, stocks deemed to be very high or low risk may be subject to higher or lower bands as they relate to the rating. When such exceptions apply, they will be identified in the Company Disclosures table in the relevant research piece.

## Company profile and fee and risk statement under the Japanese Financial Instruments & Exchange Law

Company Name etc: UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd., Financial Instruments & Exchange Firm, Kanto Local Financial Bureau (Kinsho) No.2633

Associated Memberships: Japan Securities Dealers' Association, the Financial Futures Association of Japan, and Type II Financial Instruments Firms Association

UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. will receive a brokerage fee based on an individual contract and no standard upper limit or calculating method. For the trading of domestic stocks, consumption tax is added to the fee. For the trading of foreign stock, fee on the foreign stock exchange or foreign tax may be charged in addition to the domestic fee and tax. Those amounts may vary depending on the jurisdiction. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price of the stock in the case of trading stocks, and that a loss may occur due to the exchange rate in the case of trading foreign stocks. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price or performance of the properties in the portfolio in the case of trading REITs

UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. will only receive the purchasing amounts for trading unlisted bonds (JGBs, municipals, government guaranteed bonds, corporate bonds) when UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. is the counterparty. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price of the bond caused by the fluctuations in the interest rates, and that a loss may occur due to the exchange rate in the case of trading foreign bonds.
